B_merge

LEALCO-BEH2: analyse data from test task

Information

Goal

Inputs

The output file is a large .csv table containing the entire data of the file in a long format. It puts together the PsychoPy input files, the demographics, the VOT files as well as the relevant information from the debriefing questionnaire.

Output

Read files

Read files:

  • data: file containing the main data

  • norms: file (derived from the data file) containing naming norms based on untrained trials only

# ================ read files
data <- read.csv('data/LEALCO-BEH2_test_merged_data.csv')
norms <- read.csv('data/LEALCO-BEH2_test_merged_naming_norms.csv')

Make certain variables into factors

# make factors
data$subjID <- as_factor(data$subjID)
data$partner_order <- as_factor(data$partner_order)
data$block <- as_factor(data$block)
data$partner <- as_factor(data$partner)
data$class <- as_factor(data$class)
data$type <- as_factor(data$type)
data$test_partner <- as_factor(data$test_partner)
data$target_stimID <- as_factor(data$target_stimID)
data$correct <- as_factor(data$correct)
data$error <- as_factor(data$error)
data$target_label <- as_factor(data$target_label)
data$alternative_label <- as_factor(data$alternative_label)
data$produced_label <- as_factor(data$produced_label)

# relevel
levels(data$correct) <- c("other", "matching",  "non-matching")
data$correct <- relevel(data$correct,  'non-matching')
data$correct <- relevel(data$correct,  'matching')

# relevel
data$produced_label <- relevel(data$produced_label,  'label_B')
data$produced_label <- relevel(data$produced_label,  'label_A')

# create some new variables for primacy and recency
data <- data %>% 
  mutate(primacy = case_when(partner_order == 'A - B / A - B' ~ "A",
                       partner_order == 'A - B / B - A' ~ "A",
                       partner_order == 'B - A / B - A' ~ "B",
                       partner_order == 'B - A / A - B' ~ "B"))
data$primacy <- as_factor(data$primacy)

data <- data %>% 
  mutate(recency = case_when(partner_order == 'A - B / A - B' ~ "B",
                       partner_order == 'A - B / B - A' ~ "B",
                       partner_order == 'B - A / B - A' ~ "A",
                       partner_order == 'B - A / A - B' ~ "A"))
data$recency <- as_factor(data$recency)

data <- data %>%
  mutate(training_order = case_when(partner_order == 'A - B / A - B' ~ "AB",
                       partner_order == 'A - B / B - A' ~ "AB",
                       partner_order == 'B - A / B - A' ~ "BA",
                       partner_order == 'B - A / A - B' ~ "BA"))
data$training_order <- as_factor(data$training_order)

# add naming norms for the label that was actually produced by the subject
data <- left_join(
  data,
  norms,
  by = 'target_stimID')
data <- data %>%
  mutate(norm = case_when(produced_label == 'label_A' ~ label_A_prop,
                          produced_label == 'label_B' ~ label_B_prop,
                          produced_label == 'other' ~ NA))

For each trial, create new variable containing the perceived quality of teamwork, depending on the current partner (this just takes the correct value from the debriefing questionnaire, depending on which partner the trial took place with)

# 
data$team  <- ifelse(data$partner == 'A', data$T101, data$T201)

Preliminary data sanity checks

(1) Check general error pattern

Here, the general error distribution (regardless of experimental condition) is assessed by subject. This can potentially constitute the basis for data exclusion.

Table 1: Key for interpreting the “Error”” variable
Code Meaning
correct said exactly what they were expected to say
1 compounded
2 semantically related
3 phonologically related
4 unrelated
5 misarticulation
6 other
7 visual
8 audio recording error
9 no response 
# plot accuracy counts by subject
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_raw screening.pdf")
p <- ggplot(data, aes(x=error, fill=error)) + 
  geom_bar() +
  facet_wrap(~subjID)
p

(2) Check general RT pattern

Let’s screen for anomalous RTs in the data files, which could have originated from inaccuracies in data reprocessing. It appears that no anomalies are (any longer) present in the data.

# overall density plot
plot(density(data$VOT, na.rm = TRUE))
# zooming into the left part of the histogramm to spot anomalous VOTs (<400 ms), and export them.
hist(data$VOT, xlim=c(0, 1000), breaks = 1000)
dummy <- data[data$VOT < 400,]
write.csv(dummy, 'data/LEALCO-BEH2_test_anomalous_RTs.csv', row.names=FALSE, fileEncoding = "UTF-8")

# Look for NA values and export them.
dummy <- data[is.na(data$VOT),]
write.csv(dummy, 'data/LEALCO-BEH2_test_anomalousNA_RTs.csv', row.names=FALSE, fileEncoding = "UTF-8")

# histogramm fo ronly correct responses
hist(data$VOT[data$error == 'correct'], xlim=c(0, 1000), breaks = 1000)
plot(density(data$VOT[data$error == 'correct'], na.rm = TRUE))

(3) Transform non-normal variables

After trying several normalization approaches, it appears that the inverse normalization is most appropriatel.

# plot native VOTs
hist(data$VOT, breaks=500)
# plot log10 notmalized VOTs
data$VOTlog10 <- log10(data$VOT)
hist(data$VOTlog10, breaks=500)
# plot inverse normalized VOTs
data$VOTinv <- 1/data$VOT
hist(data$VOTinv, breaks=500)
# plot square root normalized VOTs
data$VOTsqrt <- sqrt(data$VOT)
hist(data$VOTsqrt, breaks=500)

(4) Are there anomalous subjects?

Calculate descriptive across collapsed conditions. Note that no trials/subjects are excluded.

data$is_correct <- 0
data$is_correct[data$error == 'correct'] <-1


# descritpives of accuracy: within-subjects
summary_acc_wSubj <- data %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  summarise(mean_ACC = mean(is_correct), sd=sd(is_correct), sem=(sd(is_correct)/sqrt((length(is_correct)))))

# descritpives of accuracy: between-subjects
summary_acc_bSubj <- summary_acc_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grand_mean_ACC = mean(mean_ACC), sd=sd(mean_ACC), sem=(sd(mean_ACC)/sqrt((length(mean_ACC)))))

# descritpives of vot: within-subjects
summary_vot_wSubj <- data %>%
  filter(is_correct == 1) %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  summarise(mean_VOT = mean(VOT), sd=sd(VOT), sem=(sd(VOT)/sqrt((length(VOT)))))

# descritpives of vot: between-subjects
summary_vot_bSubj <- summary_vot_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grand_mean_VOT = mean(mean_VOT), sd=sd(mean_VOT), sem=(sd(mean_VOT)/sqrt((length(mean_VOT)))))

=== 2-label items ===

Subject exclusion

Select trained trials, with a focus on those that are valid, i.e. where no recording error occurred. We therefore exclude all trials coded as: (5) misarticulation, (6) other, (7) visual, (8) audio recording error (9) no response.

data_2labels <- data %>%
  filter(type == '2-label') %>%
  filter(error %in% c('correct', '1t', '1a', '2t', '2a', '3t', '3a', '4'))

Determine if any subjects need to be excluded based on data distribution for accuracy (collapsing all test conditions).

# aggregate within subject
data_2labels_sum_wSubj <- data_2labels %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(is_correct), sem=(sd(is_correct)/sqrt((length(is_correct)))), sd=sd(is_correct))

# aggregate between subjects
data_2labels_sum_bSubj <- data_2labels_sum_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

# calculate SD intervals
plus2SD = data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean + data_2labels_sum_bSubj$sd * 2
minus2SD = data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean - data_2labels_sum_bSubj$sd * 2
plus25SD = data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean + data_2labels_sum_bSubj$sd * 2.5
minus25SD = data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean - data_2labels_sum_bSubj$sd * 2.5
plus3SD = data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean + data_2labels_sum_bSubj$sd * 3
minus3SD = data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean - data_2labels_sum_bSubj$sd * 3

# plot
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_acc_outliers.pdf")
ggplot(data_2labels_sum_wSubj, aes(x=mean)) +
  geom_dotplot() +
  geom_density(alpha=.2, fill="black") +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean)) +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=plus2SD), linetype="dashed") +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=minus2SD), linetype="dashed") +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=plus25SD), linetype="dashed") +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=minus25SD), linetype="dashed") +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=plus3SD), linetype="dashed") +
  geom_vline(aes(xintercept=minus3SD), linetype="dashed") +
  annotate("text", x=plus2SD, y=3, label="+2SD", angle = 30) +
  annotate("text", x=minus2SD, y=3, label="-2SD", angle = 30) +
  annotate("text", x=plus25SD, y=3, label="+2.5SD", angle = 30) +
  annotate("text", x=minus25SD, y=3, label="-2.5SD", angle = 30) +
  annotate("text", x=plus3SD, y=3, label="+3SD", angle = 30) +
  annotate("text", x=minus3SD, y=3, label="-3SD", angle = 30) +
  annotate("text", x=data_2labels_sum_bSubj$grandMean, y=3, label="mean", angle = 30) +
  xlim(0,1) +
 ggtitle("Subject outliers based on accuracy")
Bin width defaults to 1/30 of the range of the data. Pick better value with
`binwidth`.

Overall, this condition-blind analysis of outliers showed that, for the accuracy measure, applying the following threshold results in excluding following subjects:

  • 2 SD: 15, 25

  • 2.5 SD: 15

  • 3 SD: 15

For the (native) VOT measure applying the following threshold results in excluding following subjects:

  • 2 SD: 15

  • 2.5 SD: 15

  • 3 SD: 15

For the (inverted) VOT measure applying the following threshold results in excluding following subjects:

  • 2 SD: 15

  • 2.5 SD: 15

  • 3 SD: 15

In addition, subject 20 reported not having a “normal or corrected-to-normal” hearing and has therefore been excluded from the analyses.

We select the exclusion criteria of +/-3SD resulting in the exclusion of subjects 15 and 20.

exclusion_acc <- c()
exclusion_vot <- c()

Label Maintenance

By Subject (congruency)

Here we just have a simple look at accuracy, that is we consider correct only those trials where the subject said exactly the word they were expected to say.

# aggregate
acc_wSubj_congruency <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc) %>%
  group_by(subjID, correct) %>%
  summarize(n=n()) %>%
  mutate(proportion = n / sum(n))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
# save
write.csv(acc_wSubj_congruency, "output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_accuracy_withinSubjects_congruency.csv", row.names = FALSE)

# plot (within subject)
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_accuracy_withinSubjects_congruency_barplot.pdf")
a <- ggplot(acc_wSubj_congruency, aes(y=proportion, x=correct, fill=correct)) + 
  geom_bar(stat="identity", color = "black", position=position_dodge()) +
  scale_fill_manual(values = pal3) +
  labs(y="Produced labels (proportion)", x="Label Type") +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=12), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  scale_x_discrete(guide = guide_axis(n.dodge = 2)) +
  ylim(0,1) +
  facet_wrap(~subjID)
a

By Subject (Partner x Label)

# aggregte (within subject)
acc_wSubj_partner <- data_2labels %>%
 filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc) %>%
 group_by(subjID, partner, produced_label, .drop=FALSE) %>%
  summarize(n=n())
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID', 'partner'. You can override using
the `.groups` argument.
# calculate proportions 
counts <- data_2labels %>%
 filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc) %>%
 group_by(subjID, partner,  .drop=FALSE) %>%
  summarize(n_total=n())
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
acc_wSubj_partner <- merge(x=acc_wSubj_partner,y=counts, 
             by=c("subjID", "partner"))
acc_wSubj_partner$proportion <- acc_wSubj_partner$n/acc_wSubj_partner$n_tot

# add correctness
acc_wSubj_partner$correct <- "other"
acc_wSubj_partner$correct[acc_wSubj_partner$partner == 'A' & acc_wSubj_partner$produced_label == 'label_A'] <- "matched"
acc_wSubj_partner$correct[acc_wSubj_partner$partner == 'B' & acc_wSubj_partner$produced_label == 'label_B'] <- "matched"
acc_wSubj_partner$correct[acc_wSubj_partner$partner == 'A' & acc_wSubj_partner$produced_label == 'label_A'] <- "matched"
acc_wSubj_partner$correct[acc_wSubj_partner$partner == 'B' & acc_wSubj_partner$produced_label == 'label_B'] <- "matched"
acc_wSubj_partner$correct[acc_wSubj_partner$partner == 'A' & acc_wSubj_partner$produced_label == 'label_B'] <- "non-matched"
acc_wSubj_partner$correct[acc_wSubj_partner$partner == 'B' & acc_wSubj_partner$produced_label == 'label_A'] <- "non-matched"

# plot
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_accuracy_withinSubjects_partner_barplot.pdf")
a <- ggplot(acc_wSubj_partner, aes(y=proportion, x=partner, group=produced_label, fill=produced_label)) +
  geom_bar(stat="identity", color = "black", position=position_dodge()) +
  scale_fill_manual(values =pal1) +
  labs(y="Precedent maintenance (proportion)", x="Partner") +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=15), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  ylim(0,1) +
  facet_wrap(~subjID)
a
dev.off()

# aggregte (between subject)
acc_bSubj_partner <- acc_wSubj_partner %>%
  group_by(partner, produced_label, correct) %>%
  summarise(mean_bSubj = mean(proportion), sd_bSubj=sd(proportion), sem_bSubj=(sd(proportion)/sqrt((length(proportion)))), n_bSubj=length(proportion))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'partner', 'produced_label'. You can
override using the `.groups` argument.

labels <- data.frame(
  x = c(
    seq(-0.5, -0.25, length.out = 10),
    seq(-0.5, -0.25, length.out = 11)
  ),
  label = c(

LMM (binomial)

Here, we run a first model. Note that our dependent variable (the type of label produced by the subjects) is a categorical variable with 3 levels (label A, label B, other). LMMs are not naturally made to handle DVs with more than 2 levels (but see here and here for possible solutions). For this reason, we run a first model where we “drop” the level “other”.

Prepare the ACC data for LMMs.

# for each trial, cretae new variable containing the perceived quality of teamwork, dependin gon the current partner (this just takes the correct value from the debfirefing questionnaire, depending on which partner the trial took place with)
#data_trained$team  <- ifelse(data_trained$partner == 'A', data_trained$T101, #data_trained$T201)

# select data
acc_binomial <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc) %>%
  filter(correct != 'other')

# drop levels that are not present in the data
acc_binomial$produced_label <- droplevels(acc_binomial$produced_label)

# Set sum contrasts for all variables.
contrasts(acc_binomial$test_partner) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_binomial$produced_label) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_binomial$block)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_binomial$primacy)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_binomial$training_order)  <- contr.sum(2)


# check contrasts
contrasts(acc_binomial$test_partner)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(acc_binomial$produced_label)
        [,1]
label_A    1
label_B   -1
contrasts(acc_binomial$block)
  [,1]
1    1
2   -1
contrasts(acc_binomial$primacy)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(acc_binomial$recency)
  A
B 0
A 1
contrasts(acc_binomial$training_order) 
   [,1]
AB    1
BA   -1
# center continious covariates
acc_binomial <- acc_binomial %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))

Try to find the maximally convergent model by using the buildmer() approach.

# indicate the maximal effect srucutre (both fixend and rendom) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- produced_label ~ test_partner*block * training_order * c_norm +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order | subjID) +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
m_acc <- buildmer(f,family=binomial, data=acc_binomial,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

> Post-hoc / marginal means (Test Partner)

# make post-hoc with no correction
m_acc.emm_none_partner <-emmeans(m_acc@model, pairwise ~ test_partner, adjust = "none")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
m_acc.ph_none_partner <- as.data.frame(m_acc.emm_none_partner$contrasts)
write.csv(m_acc.emm_none_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_acc_ph_none_testpartner.csv", row.names = FALSE)

LMM (pseudo-multinomial)

The “binomial” approach has the advantage that is allows to directly tets the effect of partner on label_A and label_B which is our core researh question. However, the downside is tht it requires us to completely disregard the “other” responses. A work around is th transform our trinomial DV in three separat e DV which each will be modelleld separately.

# select data
acc_multinomial <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc)

# create dummy DVs for each of the levels of our DV
acc_multinomial$dummy_label_A  <- ifelse(acc_multinomial$produced_label == 'label_A', 1, 0)
acc_multinomial$dummy_label_B  <- ifelse(acc_multinomial$produced_label == 'label_B', 1, 0)   
acc_multinomial$dummy_label_other  <- ifelse(acc_multinomial$produced_label == 'other', 1, 0)   


acc_multinomial$dummy_label_A <- as_factor(acc_multinomial$dummy_label_A)
acc_multinomial$dummy_label_B <- as_factor(acc_multinomial$dummy_label_B)
acc_multinomial$dummy_label_other <- as_factor(acc_multinomial$dummy_label_other)

# Set sum contrasts for all variables.
contrasts(acc_multinomial$test_partner) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_multinomial$block)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_multinomial$primacy)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_multinomial$recency)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(acc_multinomial$training_order)  <- contr.sum(2)

# check contrasts
contrasts(acc_multinomial$test_partner)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(acc_multinomial$block)
  [,1]
1    1
2   -1
contrasts(acc_multinomial$primacy)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(acc_multinomial$recency)
  [,1]
B    1
A   -1
contrasts(acc_multinomial$training_order)
   [,1]
AB    1
BA   -1
# center continious covariates
acc_multinomial <- acc_multinomial %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))
# indicate the maximal effect srucutre (both fixend and rendom) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- dummy_label_A ~ test_partner*block * training_order * c_norm +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | subjID) +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
m_acc_labelA <- buildmer(f,family=binomial, data=acc_multinomial,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

# indicate the maximal effect srucutre (both fixend and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- dummy_label_B ~ test_partner*block * training_order * c_norm +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order | subjID) +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
m_acc_labelB <- buildmer(f,family=binomial, data=acc_multinomial,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

# indicate the maximal effect srucutre (both fixend and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- dummy_label_other ~ test_partner*block * training_order +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order | subjID) +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
m_acc_labelother <- buildmer(f,family=binomial, data=acc_multinomial,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

> Post-hoc / marginal means (Test Partner)

# ========= LABEL A ========

# make post-hoc with no correction
m_acc_labelA.emm_none_partner <-emmeans(m_acc_labelA@model, pairwise ~ test_partner, adjust = "none")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
m_acc_labelA.ph_none_partner <- as.data.frame(m_acc_labelA.emm_none_partner$contrasts)
write.csv(m_acc_labelA.emm_none_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_acc_labelA_ph_none_testpartner.csv", row.names = FALSE)
Warning in as.data.frame.emm_list(x[[i]], optional = TRUE, stringsAsFactors = stringsAsFactors): Note: 'as.data.frame' has combined your 2 sets of results into one object,
and this affects things like adjusted P values. Refer to the annotations.
# extract and save marginal means
m_acc_labelA.mm_partner <- as.data.frame(m_acc_labelA.emm_none_partner$emmeans)
m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$emmean_untrans <- plogis(m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$emmean)
m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$asymp.LCL_untrans <- plogis(m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$asymp.LCL)
m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$asymp.UCL_untrans <- plogis(m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$asymp.UCL)

# save them
write.csv(m_acc_labelA.mm_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_acc_lmm_marginalMeans_labelA_partner.csv", row.names = FALSE)

# ======== visualize =========

m_acc_labelA.mm_partner$produced_label <- 'label_A'
m_acc_labelB.mm_partner$produced_label <- 'label_B'
m_acc_labelother.mm_partner$produced_label <- 'other'

m_acc_all.mm_partner <- rbind(m_acc_labelA.mm_partner, m_acc_labelB.mm_partner, m_acc_labelother.mm_partner)

m_acc_all.mm_partner <- m_acc_all.mm_partner %>% 
  mutate(match = case_when(test_partner == 'A' & produced_label == 'label_A' ~ "matched",
                           test_partner == 'B' & produced_label == 'label_B' ~ "matched",
                           test_partner == 'A' & produced_label == 'label_B' ~ "non-matched",
                           test_partner == 'B' & produced_label == 'label_A' ~ "non-matched",
                           produced_label == 'other' ~ 'other'))

# visualize marginal means
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_acc_lmm_marginal_labelall_partner_pointplot.pdf")
tt <- ggplot(m_acc_all.mm_partner, aes(y=emmean_untrans, x=test_partner, group=produced_label, col=produced_label)) +
  scale_colour_manual(values = pal1) +
  geom_point(stat="identity", position=position_dodge(0.5), size=3) +
  geom_errorbar(aes(ymin=asymp.LCL_untrans, ymax=asymp.UCL_untrans), width=.2, position=position_dodge(.5)) +
  labs(y="Precedent maintenance\n(proportion)", x="Partner") +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=12), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  ylim(0,1) 
tt

> Post-hoc / marginal means (Test Partner x Norm x Training Order)

Naming latencies at correct trials

By Subject (congruency)

Here we just have a simple look at naming latencies, while considering only trials that are correct. In a first moment, we just examine naming latencies in their native format, that is without any type of normalization for approximating normality. Note that the data is right-skewed, expected for latency data.

# aggregate
vot_wSubj_congruency <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(error =='correct') %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_vot) %>%
  group_by(subjID, correct) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(VOT), sd=sd(VOT), sem=(sd(VOT)/sqrt((length(VOT)))), n=length(VOT))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
# save
write.csv(vot_wSubj_congruency, "output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_voz_withinSubjects_congruent.csv", row.names = FALSE)

# plot (within subject)
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_vot_withinSubjects_congruent_barplot.pdf")
a <- ggplot(vot_wSubj_congruency, aes(y=mean, x=correct, fill=correct)) + 
  geom_bar(stat="identity", color = "black", position=position_dodge()) +
  scale_fill_manual(values = pal3) +
  labs(y="Naming latencies (ms)", x="Label Type") +
  geom_errorbar(aes(ymin=mean-sem, ymax=mean+sem), width=.2, position=position_dodge(.9)) +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=12), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  scale_x_discrete(guide = guide_axis(n.dodge = 2)) +
  ylim(0,NA) +
  facet_wrap(~subjID)
a
dev.off() 
png 
  2 
a
# aggregte (between subject)
vot_bSubj_congruency <- vot_wSubj_congruency %>%
  group_by(correct) %>%
  summarise(mean_bSubj = mean(mean), sd_bSubj=sd(mean), sem_bSubj=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), n_bSubj=length(mean))

# save

  geom_bar(stat="identity", color = "black", position=position_dodge()) +
  geom_errorbar(aes(ymin=mean_bSubj-sem_bSubj, ymax=mean_bSubj+sem_bSubj), width=.2, position=position_dodge(.9)) +

  labs(y="Naming latencies (ms)", x="Label Type") +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=15), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  theme(legend.title = element_blank()) +
  ylim(0,NA)

  geom_text(aes(label = produced_label), vjust = -2.5, position=position_dodge(0.9))

dev.off()

tt

LMM

Prepare the VOT data for LMMs. Note that we will be running he model on inverted naming latency (1/naming latency). This results in the magnitude of the datapoints being inverted. The estimates of the models have to be read accordingly.

# select data
votINV <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(error == 'correct') %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_vot)

# drop levels that are not present in the data
votINV$produced_label <- droplevels(votINV$produced_label)

# Set sum contrasts for all variables.
contrasts(votINV$test_partner) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(votINV$produced_label) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(votINV$block)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(votINV$primacy)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(votINV$recency)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(votINV$training_order)  <- contr.sum(2)

# check contrasts
contrasts(votINV$test_partner)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(votINV$produced_label)
        [,1]
label_A    1
label_B   -1
contrasts(votINV$block)
  [,1]
1    1
2   -1
contrasts(votINV$primacy)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(votINV$recency)
  [,1]
B    1
A   -1
contrasts(votINV$training_order)
   [,1]
AB    1
BA   -1
# center continious covariates
votINV <- votINV %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))

Try to find the maximally convergent model by using the buildmer() approach.

# indicate the maximal effect strucutre (both fixed and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- VOTinv ~ test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order * c_norm +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | subjID) +
     (test_partner*produced_label*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
m_votINV <- buildmer(f,data=votINV,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

Post-hoc / marginal means for (Partner Type x Label)

Compute post-hoc tests and export.

# make post-hoc with tukey correction
m_votINV.emm_tukey_partner_x_label <-emmeans(m_votINV@model, pairwise ~ test_partner * produced_label, adjust = "tukey")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
m_votINV.ph_tukey_partner_x_label <- as.data.frame(m_votINV.emm_tukey_partner_x_label$contrasts)
write.csv(m_votINV.ph_tukey_partner_x_label,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_votINV_ph_tukey_partner_x_label.csv", row.names = FALSE)

# make post-hoc with no correction
m_votINV.emm_none_partner_x_label <-emmeans(m_votINV@model, pairwise ~ test_partner * produced_label, adjust = "none")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
m_votINV.ph_none_partner_x_label <- as.data.frame(m_votINV.emm_none_partner_x_label$contrasts)
m_votINV.ph_none_partner_x_label$p.valueBonf2 = m_votINV.ph_none_partner_x_label$p.value *2
m_votINV.ph_none_partner_x_label$p.valueBonf4 = m_votINV.ph_none_partner_x_label$p.value *4
write.csv(m_votINV.ph_none_partner_x_label,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_votINV_ph_none_partner_x_label.csv", row.names = FALSE)

# extract and save marginal means
m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label <- as.data.frame(m_votINV.emm_none_partner_x_label$emmeans)
m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label$emmean_untrans <- 1/m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label$emmean
m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label$lower.CL_untrans <- 1/m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label$upper.CL
m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label$upper.CL_untrans <- 1/m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label$lower.CL
m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label <- m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label %>% 
  mutate(match = case_when(test_partner == 'A' & produced_label == 'label_A' ~ "match",
                           test_partner == 'B' & produced_label == 'label_B' ~ "match",
                           test_partner == 'A' & produced_label == 'label_B' ~ "non-match",
                           test_partner == 'B' & produced_label == 'label_A' ~ "non-match"))
write.csv(m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_votINV_lmm_marginal_partner_x_block.csv", row.names = FALSE)

# plot
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_votINV_lmm_marginal_partner_x_label_pointplot.pdf")
tt <- ggplot(m_votINV.mm_partner_x_label, aes(y=emmean_untrans, x=test_partner, group = produced_label, col = produced_label)) +
  scale_colour_manual(values = pal1) +
  geom_point(stat="identity", position=position_dodge(.9), size=3) +
  geom_errorbar(aes(ymin=lower.CL_untrans, ymax=upper.CL_untrans), width=.2, position=position_dodge(.9)) +
  labs(y="Naming latencies (ms)", x="Partner") +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=12), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  ylim(0,2500) +
  geom_text(aes(label = produced_label), vjust = -8, position=position_dodge(0.9), col='black')
tt
dev.off()

Entrainment analysis

The following analysis examines the reaction between Label Maintenance and (inverted) Naming Latency. This is interesting because there could be a speed accuracy trade-off (i.e. entraining with the partner is cognitively costly) or on the contrary it is possible that entraining with the partner is in fact facilitatory.

# aggregte accuracy (within subject)
acc_wSubj_match <- acc_wSubj_congruency %>%
  filter(correct %in% c('matching', 'non-matching'))
acc_wSubj_match$n <- NULL

# aggregate VOTinv (within subjects) only for correct trials
votINV_wSubj_match <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(error == 'correct') %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_vot) %>%
  group_by(subjID, correct) %>%
  summarise(mean_VOTinv = mean(VOTinv))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
# merge the two DVs in one dataframe
entrainment_data <- merge(acc_wSubj_match,votINV_wSubj_match,by=c("subjID","correct"))

# pivot from long to wide
entrainment_data <- entrainment_data %>% 
  pivot_wider(
    names_from = correct, 
    values_from = c(proportion, mean_VOTinv)
  )

# calculate entrainment scores (same - different)
entrainment_data$entrainment_ACC <- entrainment_data$proportion_matching - entrainment_data$`proportion_non-matching`
entrainment_data$entrainment_VOTinv <- (entrainment_data$mean_VOTinv_matching - entrainment_data$`mean_VOTinv_non-matching`) * 10000

# scatterplot
ggplot(entrainment_data, aes(x=entrainment_VOTinv, y=entrainment_ACC)) +
  geom_point() +
  stat_smooth(method=lm, fullrange=TRUE, color = 'black') + 
  geom_hline(yintercept=0, linetype="dotted") +
  geom_vline(xintercept=0, linetype="dotted") +
  xlim(-5, 5) +
  ylim(-1.5, 1.5) + 
  xlab('<- faster for different                                                              faster for same ->\n\nSpeed advantage\n(same - different)') +
  ylab('Precedent maintenance advantage\n(same - different)\n\n<-higher for different             higher for same ->')
`geom_smooth()` using formula = 'y ~ x'
ggsave("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_entrainment_correlation.pdf")
Saving 7 x 5 in image
`geom_smooth()` using formula = 'y ~ x'
# correlation
corr <- cor.test(entrainment_data$entrainment_ACC, entrainment_data$entrainment_VOTinv)
sink('output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_entrainment_correlation.txt')
print(corr)

Error patterns at inaccurate trials

By Subject (native, partner x block)

First, we need to dummy code our error variable so that from an error variable with 4 levels, we get four variables (one per error type) with a binary outcome each. This will make it easier to calculate rates for each error type as well as run LMM later.

=== 1-label items ===

data_1label <- data %>%
  filter(type == '1-label') %>%
  filter(error %in% c('correct', '1t', '1a', '2t', '2a', '3t', '3a', '4'))

Label Maintenance

# aggregate within subject
label1_acc_wSubj <- data_1label %>%
  filter(!subjID %in% exclusion_acc) %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(is_correct), sem=(sd(is_correct)/sqrt((length(is_correct)))), sd=sd(is_correct))

# aggregate between subjects
label1_acc_bSubj <- label1_acc_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

# aggregate within subject
label1_acc_wSubj_partner <- data_1label %>%
  group_by(subjID, test_partner) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(is_correct), sem=(sd(is_correct)/sqrt((length(is_correct)))), sd=sd(is_correct))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
# aggregate between subjects
label1_acc_bSubj_partner <- label1_acc_wSubj_partner %>%
  group_by(test_partner) %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

LMM

# select data
label1_acc <- data_1label %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc)

# Set sum contrasts for all variables.
contrasts(label1_acc$test_partner) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label1_acc$block)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label1_acc$primacy)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label1_acc$training_order)  <- contr.sum(2)


# check contrasts
contrasts(label1_acc$test_partner)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(label1_acc$block)
  [,1]
1    1
2   -1
contrasts(label1_acc$primacy)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(label1_acc$recency)
  A
B 0
A 1
contrasts(label1_acc$training_order) 
   [,1]
AB    1
BA   -1
# center continious covariates
label1_acc <- label1_acc %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))
# indicate the maximal effect srucutre (both fixend and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- is_correct ~ test_partner*block * training_order +
     (test_partner * block * training_order | subjID) +
     (test_partner * block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
label1_m_acc <- buildmer(f,family=binomial, data=label1_acc,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

# make post-hoc with no correction
label1_m_acc.emm_none_partner <-emmeans(label1_m_acc@model, pairwise ~ test_partner, adjust = "none")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
label1_m_acc.ph_none_partner <- as.data.frame(label1_m_acc.emm_none_partner$contrasts)
write.csv(label1_m_acc.emm_none_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_label1_acc_ph_none_testpartner.csv", row.names = FALSE)

and this affects things like adjusted P values. Refer to the annotations.
# extract and save marginal means
label1_m_acc.mm_partner <- as.data.frame(label1_m_acc.emm_none_partner$emmeans)
label1_m_acc.mm_partner$emmean_untrans <- plogis(label1_m_acc.mm_partner$emmean)

Naming Latency

# aggregate within subject
label1_vot_wSubj <- data_1label %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  filter(subjID %in% exclusion_vot) %>%
  filter(error == 'correct') %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(VOT), sem=(sd(VOT)/sqrt((length(VOT)))), sd=sd(VOT))

# aggregate between subjects
label1_vot_bSubj <- label1_vot_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

# aggregate within subject
label1_vot_wSubj_partner <- data_1label %>%
  group_by(subjID, test_partner) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(VOT), sem=(sd(VOT)/sqrt((length(VOT)))), sd=sd(VOT))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
# aggregate between subjects
label1_vot_bSubj_partner <- label1_vot_wSubj_partner %>%
  group_by(test_partner) %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

LMM

# select data
label1_vot <- data_2labels %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_vot) %>%
  filter (error == 'correct')

# Set sum contrasts for all variables.
contrasts(label1_vot$test_partner) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label1_vot$block)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label1_vot$primacy)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label1_vot$training_order)  <- contr.sum(2)


# check contrasts

# center continious covariates
label1_vot <- label1_vot %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))
# indicate the maximal effect strucutre (both fixed and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- VOTinv ~ test_partner * block * training_order * c_norm +
     (test_partner*block * training_order  | subjID) +
     (test_partner*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
label1_m_votINV <- buildmer(f,data=label1_vot,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

# make post-hoc with no correction
label1_m_votINV.emm_none_partner <-emmeans(label1_m_votINV@model, pairwise ~ test_partner, adjust = "none")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
label1_m_votINV.ph_none_partner <- as.data.frame(label1_m_votINV.emm_none_partner$contrasts)
write.csv(label1_m_votINV.ph_none_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_label1_votINV_ph_none_partner.csv", row.names = FALSE)


# extract and save marginal means
label1_m_votINV.mm_partner <- as.data.frame(label1_m_votINV.emm_none_partner$emmeans)
label1_m_votINV.mm_partner$emmean_untrans <- 1/label1_m_votINV.mm_partner$emmean
label1_m_votINV.mm_partner$lower.CL_untrans <- 1/label1_m_votINV.mm_partner$upper.CL
label1_m_votINV.mm_partner$upper.CL_untrans <- 1/label1_m_votINV.mm_partner$lower.CL
write.csv(label1_m_votINV.mm_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_label1_votINV_lmm_marginal_partner.csv", row.names = FALSE)


# plot
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_label1_votINV_lmm_marginal_partner_pointplot.pdf")
tt <- ggplot(label1_m_votINV.mm_partner, aes(y=emmean_untrans, x=test_partner, color=test_partner)) +
  #scale_colour_manual(values = pal1) +
  geom_point(stat="identity", position=position_dodge(.9), size=3, col='black') +
  geom_errorbar(aes(ymin=lower.CL_untrans, ymax=upper.CL_untrans), width=.2, position=position_dodge(.9), col='black') +
  labs(y="Naming latencies (ms)", x="Partner") +

  ylim(0,2500)

=== 0-label items (untrained items) ===

data_0label <- data %>%
  filter(type == 'untrained') %>%
  filter(error %in% c('correct', '1t', '1a', '2t', '2a', '3t', '3a', '4'))

Accuracy

# aggregate within subject
label0_acc_wSubj <- data_0label %>%
  filter(!subjID %in% exclusion_acc) %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(is_correct), sem=(sd(is_correct)/sqrt((length(is_correct)))), sd=sd(is_correct))

# aggregate between subjects
label0_acc_bSubj <- label0_acc_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

# aggregate within subject
label0_acc_wSubj_partner <- data_0label %>%
  group_by(subjID, test_partner) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(is_correct), sem=(sd(is_correct)/sqrt((length(is_correct)))), sd=sd(is_correct))
`summarise()` has grouped output by 'subjID'. You can override using the
`.groups` argument.
# aggregate between subjects
label0_acc_bSubj_partner <- label0_acc_wSubj_partner %>%
  group_by(test_partner) %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

LMM

# select data
label0_acc <- data_0label %>%
  filter(!subjID %in%  exclusion_acc)

# Set sum contrasts for all variables.
contrasts(label0_acc$test_partner) <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label0_acc$block)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label0_acc$primacy)  <- contr.sum(2)
contrasts(label0_acc$training_order)  <- contr.sum(2)


# check contrasts
contrasts(label0_acc$test_partner)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1

  [,1]
1    1
2   -1
contrasts(label0_acc$primacy)
  [,1]
A    1
B   -1
contrasts(label0_acc$recency)
  A
B 0
A 1
contrasts(label0_acc$training_order) 
   [,1]
AB    1
BA   -1
# center continious covariates
label0_acc <- label0_acc %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))

Note that the c_norm variable must be dropped here because it has values only for the “correct” responses. Therefore it cannot be used a predictor for whether a response was correct or not.

# indicate the maximal effect srucutre (both fixend and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- is_correct ~ test_partner*block * training_order +
     (test_partner * block * training_order | subjID) +
     (test_partner * block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
label0_m_acc <- buildmer(f,family=binomial, data=label0_acc,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

By Subjects (native, frequency x block)

# aggregate within subject
label0_vot_wSubj <- data_0label %>%
  group_by(subjID) %>%
  filter(subjID %in% exclusion_vot) %>%
  filter(error == 'correct') %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(VOT), sem=(sd(VOT)/sqrt((length(VOT)))), sd=sd(VOT))

# aggregate between subjects
label0_vot_bSubj <- label0_vot_wSubj %>%
  summarise(grandMean = mean(mean), sem=(sd(mean)/sqrt((length(mean)))), sd=sd(mean))

# aggregate within subject
label0_vot_wSubj_partner <- data_0label %>%
  group_by(subjID, test_partner) %>%
  summarise(mean = mean(VOT), sem=(sd(VOT)/sqrt((length(VOT)))), sd=sd(VOT))

# center continious covariates
label0_vot <- label0_vot %>%
  mutate(c_norm = norm - mean(norm, na.rm=TRUE))
# indicate the maximal effect strucutre (both fixed and random) that is motivated based on design & hypotheses
f <- VOTinv ~ test_partner * block * training_order * c_norm +
     (test_partner*block * training_order  | subjID) +
     (test_partner*block * training_order  | target_label)

# select the maximal model that still converges
label0_m_votINV <- buildmer(f,data=label0_vot,buildmerControl=buildmerControl(direction='order',
          args=list(control=lmerControl(optimizer='bobyqa'))))

Post-hoc / marginal means

# make post-hoc with no correction
label0_m_votINV.emm_none_partner <-emmeans(label0_m_votINV@model, pairwise ~ test_partner, adjust = "none")
NOTE: Results may be misleading due to involvement in interactions
label0_m_votINV.ph_none_partner <- as.data.frame(label0_m_votINV.emm_none_partner$contrasts)
write.csv(label0_m_votINV.ph_none_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_label0_votINV_ph_none_partner.csv", row.names = FALSE)


# extract and save marginal means
label0_m_votINV.mm_partner <- as.data.frame(label0_m_votINV.emm_none_partner$emmeans)
label0_m_votINV.mm_partner$emmean_untrans <- 1/label0_m_votINV.mm_partner$emmean
label0_m_votINV.mm_partner$lower.CL_untrans <- 1/label0_m_votINV.mm_partner$upper.CL
label0_m_votINV.mm_partner$upper.CL_untrans <- 1/label0_m_votINV.mm_partner$lower.CL
write.csv(label0_m_votINV.mm_partner,"output_stats/LEALCO-BEH2_label0_votINV_lmm_marginal_partner.csv", row.names = FALSE)


# plot
pdf("output_figures/LEALCO-BEH2_label0_votINV_lmm_marginal_partner_pointplot.pdf")
tt <- ggplot(label0_m_votINV.mm_partner, aes(y=emmean_untrans, x=test_partner, color=test_partner)) +
  #scale_colour_manual(values = pal1) +
  geom_point(stat="identity", position=position_dodge(.9), size=3, col='black') +
  geom_errorbar(aes(ymin=lower.CL_untrans, ymax=upper.CL_untrans), width=.2, position=position_dodge(.9), col='black') +
  labs(y="Naming latencies (ms)", x="Partner") +
  theme(axis.text=element_text(size=12), axis.title=element_text(size=16)) +
  ylim(0,2500)
tt
dev.off()
